{"type":"message_update","usage":{"input":70,"output":1523,"cacheRead":3072,"cacheWrite":0,"reasoning":0,"totalTokens":4665,"cost":{"input":0,"output":0,"cacheRead":0,"cacheWrite":0,"total":0}},"assistantMessageEvent":{"type":"thinking_end","contentIndex":0,"content":"\nThe `column_totals` function computes totals per column, but doesn't handle text cells (as seen in a test `test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells`). But we need a totals row, not column totals. The test `test_export_appends_totals_row` expects the last row to be \"TOTAL,4,6\" for input [[1,2],[3,4]]. That is, the totals row contains the word \"TOTAL\" in the first column, and then the sum of each column? Actually the expected output is \"TOTAL,4,6\". The first row is \"1,2\", second is \"3,4\", third is \"TOTAL,4,6\". So the totals row has \"TOTAL\" in the first column, and then the total of each column (2+2=4? Wait column totals: column 0: 1+3=4, column 1: 2+4=6). So the totals row is [\"TOTAL\", 4, 6]. But the expected string is \"TOTAL,4,6\". So the first cell is the string \"TOTAL\", and the rest are the column sums.\n\nThus, we need to modify the export functionality to append a totals row when requested. The `export_csv` function currently doesn't take a `totals` parameter. But the test `test_export_appends_totals_row` suggests it should. Let's look at the test again:\n\n```python\ndef test_export_appends_totals_row():\n    assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n```\n\nSo `export_csv` is called with two arguments: the rows and `totals=True`. But the current implementation of `export_csv` in `export.py` is:\n\n```python\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n```\n\nIt only takes `rows`. So we need to modify `export_csv` to accept an optional `totals` parameter, and if `totals` is True, compute the totals row and append it.\n\nAlternatively, maybe `render_rows` should be responsible for rendering the totals row if a flag is set. But the test `test_render_marks_last_row_as_total` suggests that `render_rows` can mark the last row as total with `mark_last=True`. However, the current `render_rows` doesn't support that either.\n\nMaybe the intended design is: `export_csv` calls `render_rows(rows, totals=True)` or something. Wait, there are four function definitions, but the file has:\n\n```python\nfrom export import export_csv\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\n\ndef test_export_without_totals():\n    assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]]) == \"1,2\\n3,4\"\n\n\ndef test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells():\n    assert column_totals([[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]) == [\"\", 3]\n\n\ndef test_render_marks_last_row_as_total():\n    assert render_rows([[1, 2], [3, 4]], mark_last=True) == [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"]\n\n\ndef test_export_appends_totals_row():\n    assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n```\n\nSo `render_rows` is expected to take a `mark_last` parameter, and `export_csv` is expected to take a `totals` parameter.
